## [2026-06-04T20:12:55Z] assistant_thought (afterAgentThought)

I now have a solid picture of the current state. Next I will check the district map and count its interactions. I will also determine whether organize_phase should be a third deck.

The pre-refugee phase spans roughly 26 months through January 2302. Key events include the September convention, migration card, cable break, Dundalk, and orc marriages. The refugee wave begins February 2302 with refugees on Titan, Earth, and Otherworld. The abandoned ballroom triggers after month four, and the aurora pyramid appears August onward. A 20–30 card set would split these mechanics across both phases.

Refugee wave mechanics would trigger when third_wave_on_society is active or the year reaches 2302. The existing mechanics deck contains roughly five to six usable cards, excluding broken or orphaned entries. Sub-choices within those cards multiply player options without adding full cards. I am now estimating the effort required to build twenty to thirty new mechanics cards.

## [2026-06-04T20:13:16Z] assistant (afterAgentResponse)

Here’s a concise inventory of what’s in the repo today and what it would take to reach **20–30 mechanics cards** focused on **pre-refugee-wave** and **refugee-wave** play (excluding advisors and background).

---

## What “exists” today

### Core loop (working skeleton)
- **Start:** `root.scene.dry` → setup runtimes → `organize_phase_start` (sets `organizing_unlock = 1`, `phase = 1`) → `main`.
- **Month advance:** pinned `advance` → `post_event` → optional `#event` queue → back to `main`.
- **Decks on main:** Party Affairs (`#party_affairs`), Government Affairs (`#govt_affairs`), plus a duplicate “Corp/Syndicate Violence” deck that also pulls `#govt_affairs`.
- **Advisors (4 pinned):** Jacobs, Stacy, Callista, Sage — not counted toward your target.
- **Background / silent:** `mikasa_journey_inbound`, `corp_conflict`, `drone_vote_buying` — not player mechanics cards.

### Playable mechanics cards (in decks, `.scene.dry`)

| Card | Deck tag | Phase relevance | Maturity |
|------|----------|-----------------|----------|
| Municipal AI | `party_affairs` | Pre-wave | Strong (3 real choices + stacks) |
| Media ideology | `party_affairs` | Pre-wave | Strong |
| Otherworld migrations | `party_affairs` | Pre-wave only (`year = 2300`, `month < 6`) | One-shot, thin outcomes |
| Security | `govt_affairs` | Pre-wave | Medium (some `TBD` / broken gates) |
| Militia degrade | `govt_affairs` | Pre-wave | Thin |
| Shuffle Cabinet | `govt_affairs` | N/A | **Legacy Weimar** (`SPD`, `reichswehr`) — effectively dead content |

**Count in active decks: ~5–6**, not 20–30.

### Organizing cards (exist but are **not in any deck**)

Five cards under `source/scenes/cards/organizing/` with `tags: organize_phase` and `view_if: organizing_unlock = 1`:

- Education, Activist training, Contracts, Cooperatives, Magic policy  

`main.scene.dry` never includes `#organize_phase`, so these **do not appear in the hand** unless something else pulls them (nothing does). Electorate hooks for their stacks are already stubbed in `electorate_runtime.scene.dry`.

Most are **choice stubs**: increment a stack (`vocational_track`, `coop_markets`, etc.) with little or no prose, costs, or economic hooks.

### Orphaned / non-compiled content
- `party_affairs/community_outreach.old` — **5 community-outreach choices** (first/second wave, drones, otherworlders). Filename means it is **not** part of the `.scene.dry` build. That’s ~1 card worth of mechanics sitting offline.

### Scheduled **events** (not deck cards; `#event` at month-end)

Rough timeline:

| When | Content |
|------|---------|
| **2300 pre-wave** | Sept convention (Oct); Otherworld migrations card (H1); peer/party play |
| **2301 pre-wave** | Cable break / coalition (Jun); Dundalk affair (Nov); Orc marriages (Oct–Dec) |
| **2302 refugee wave** | Titan (Feb) → sets `third_wave_on_society`; Earth (Mar); Otherworld (Apr) |
| **2302+ wave fallout** | Abandoned ballroom (after May); Aurora pyramid (Aug+) |
| **2305+** | Great Protector arc (far future) |
| **Disabled** | Clan/otherworld secret arms (`0 = 1`) |

So “refugee wave” is implemented mainly as **3 major events + 2 housing events**, not as a deck of recurring mechanics cards.

### Economic / industry tangents (not card deck yet)
- `industry_list.txt` — design roadmap (~30 industry tags, self-sufficiency, war industries).
- `industries/mining.scene.dry`, `reflector.scene.dry` — district sub-scenes, partial (`placeholder_metal_demand`, reflector choices “Options to do:”).
- Heavy runtime: `economic_assets_runtime`, `organize_phase_tick` (prosperity mesh), elections — **simulation is ahead of player-facing cards**.

### Phase flags
- `phase` is set to **1** once at organize start; tick only uses `Q.phase` 1–4 for `trade_level`. **Nothing advances to phase 2–4.**
- Refugee wave gate in play: **`third_wave_on_society`** (0 until Feb 2302 Titan event). No separate `pre_refugee_wave` flag.

---

## Gap to 20–30 mechanics cards

| Bucket | Today | Target | Shortfall |
|--------|-------|--------|-----------|
| Pre-refugee mechanics cards (2300 – Jan 2302) | ~5–6 in deck + 5 unwired organize + 5 in `.old` | ~12–15 | **~7–10 net new** (after wiring/fixing) |
| Refugee-wave mechanics cards (2302+) | ~0 recurring cards; events only | ~12–15 | **~12–15 new** |
| **Total non-advisor mechanics cards** | **~10–16 if you count unwired/orphan** | **20–30** | **~15–20 authored cards** |

“Mechanics card” here means: `is-card: true`, player-drawn or repeatable policy, with `on-arrival` stacks and ideally electorate/prosperity/asset consequences — not one-shot `#event` banners.

---

## What it would take (practical scope)

### 1. Decide the delivery shape (small architectural choice)
Pick one and stick to it:

- **A. Third deck** on `main`: e.g. “Organizing” or “City policy” with `- #organize_phase` (and maybe split tags `pre_wave` / `refugee_wave`).
- **B. Fold into existing decks** with `view-if` / `view_if`: pre-wave cards in `#party_affairs` / `#govt_affairs`, refugee cards gated on `third_wave_on_society = 1`.

Right now **A is half-built** (5 organize cards + catalog modifiers) but **not wired**.

### 2. Phase gating (lightweight)
Add explicit gates so cards don’t blur together:

```dry
view-if: third_wave_on_society = 0   // pre-wave pool
view-if: third_wave_on_society = 1   // refugee-wave pool
```

Optional: set `phase = 2` on first refugee major event if you want `organize_phase_tick` trade modifiers to match narrative.

### 3. Per-card authoring checklist (~1–2 hours each at current quality)
For each of ~15–20 new cards (+ finishing stubs):

1. **`.scene.dry`** — title, `card-image`, tag, `view-if`, 2–4 subchoices with `choose-if` / `unavailable-subtitle` where needed.
2. **Stack qualities** — `on-arrival: foo += 1` (pattern already used everywhere).
3. **`electorate_runtime.scene.dry`** — one `ElectorateModifier` row per stack (organize section is the template; comment says “placeholder; tune when cards are written”).
4. **Optional hooks** — `Q.addHome` / `Q.addAsset` for housing/industry cards (ballroom/pyramid are the reference).
5. **Art** — `out/html/img/*.webp` 240×300 per card (several cards still share `asteroid.jpg`).

### 4. Revive or replace dead content (quick wins)
- Rename/restore **`community_outreach`** from `.old` → `.scene.dry` and tag pre-wave → **+1 solid card** (5 subchoices).
- **Wire the 5 organize cards** + flesh out prose/effects → **+5 cards** without new files.
- **Fix or delete** `shuffle_cabinet` and security `TBD` gates so govt deck isn’t fake depth.
- **Refugee-wave starters** (high leverage, match existing events):
  - Emergency housing / slack (feeds `economic_assets_runtime`)
  - Refugee prep vs dread follow-ups (`jovian_refugee_prepare` / `jovian_refugee_dread` already have modifier rows)
  - Third-wave outreach (parallel to orphaned community outreach)
  - 2–3 **import-substitution** cards from `industry_list.txt` (internal demand / substitution), gated post-wave or ramping pre-wave

### 5. What you can defer to stay “core”
Aligned with stepping back from tangents:

| Defer | Keep for core 20–30 |
|-------|---------------------|
| Great Protector 2305+, secret arms (`0 = 1`), war-industry rebellion chain | Party/govt/organize cards through 2302 |
| Full `industry_list.txt` asset tagging system | 3–5 industry cards + mining/reflector finished |
| District fluff scenes (7× “return to main”) | Pitts mining as 1 exemplar |
| Game_over / Weimar achievements | — |
| More lore events | Re-skin 2–3 existing events as **repeatable** cards if you need count without new lore |

### 6. Rough effort (solo design+implement)
| Task | Order of magnitude |
|------|-------------------|
| Wire deck + phase gates + `phase = 2` on wave | 0.5 day |
| Finish 5 organize stubs + electorate tune | 1–2 days |
| Restore community outreach | 0.5 day |
| **15 new cards** (balanced pre/refugee, prose + modifiers) | **2–3 weeks** at municipal_ai quality |
| **15 new cards** (minimal stubs like contracting today) | **3–5 days** but weak gameplay |
| Industry integration (tags → assets → self-sufficiency) | Separate milestone; not required for card count but needed for industry *cards* to matter |

---

## Suggested “core focus” slice (if you want one line of scope)

**Pre-wave (2300–2301):** party building, corp relations, police/militia, media, community outreach, education/contracts/coop/magic, 2–3 economy/self-sufficiency cards, fix security — **~12–15 cards**.

**Refugee wave (2302+):** prep/dread, housing pressure, third-wave outreach, ballroom/pyramid *mechanics* follow-ups (not only one-shot events), 2–3 industry/housing cards — **~12–15 cards**.

That hits **24–30** without new advisors, without 2305+ arcs, and reuses infrastructure you already paid for (electorate catalog, assets API, `third_wave_on_society`, major event timeline).

---

## Biggest surprises from the survey

1. **Organize-phase cards are authored but not playable** — no `#organize_phase` in `main`.
2. **`community_outreach` is written but not compiled** (`.old`).
3. **Refugee “wave” is event-driven**, not a card phase — only a flag and a few housing events.
4. **`industry_list.txt` is design-only**; only mining/reflector touch it, and they aren’t deck cards.
5. Several “cards” are **placeholders** (organize subchoices, shuffle cabinet, rebellion TODOs).

If you want to go narrower next, the highest ROI step is: **wire `#organize_phase` (or merge into party deck), restore community outreach, add `third_wave_on_society` gates, then batch-write refugee-wave cards that hook `addHome` and existing stacks** — before expanding industry tagging or 2305 plotlines.

The third_wave demographic exists in the election data but may have zero population until the third wave. District populations are tracked separately.

Party support is measured by projected vote percentages and seat counts from competitiveScores after modifiers. The five parties are C, G, D, H, and I, with D representing democrats. Currently active mechanics include permanent global shifts, stratum and district baseline scores, and IG membership floors. Parochialism only affects xenophobia once it exceeds 41, so it is inactive now.

## [2026-06-04T21:11:02Z] assistant (afterAgentResponse)

Here’s how **party support** works in the current build, and which mechanics actually move it.

## How “party support” is calculated

Every month end (and when you open Status → Polls), `Q.update_projections()` runs in `post_event.scene.dry`. That:

1. Rebuilds **competitive scores** from stratum/district/global baselines + legacy vote “appeals”
2. Runs **`Q.applyElectorateModifiers()`** — adds `delta × stack` from story choices onto those scores
3. Tabulates votes → **`poll_exec_*`** (citywide %) and stratum **`rich_poll_D`** etc., plus projected seats **`Proj_*`**

Parties: **D** (democrats/reform), **C** (corp), **G** (gang), **H** (hate), **I** (independents).

Story choices almost never touch polls directly; they bump **stack qualities** (e.g. `dundalk_reform += 1`), and the catalog in `electorate_runtime.scene.dry` turns stacks into score shifts.

---

## Always on (no player action)

| Source | Effect |
|--------|--------|
| **Stratum baselines** (`buildDemographics` in `election_runtime.scene.dry`) | e.g. coop leans **D** (+1) vs **C** (−1.5); drones lean **C** (+1.5); rich lean **C** (+1) |
| **District baselines** | e.g. Vats **C** +0.8; Pitts **G** +0.5 |
| **`Q.global_shifts`** | Permanent global appeals: **Ind −20**, **Gang −1**, **Hate −3** (via vote-label sync) |
| **Interest-group floors** | IG membership claims part of each block’s vote before competitive swing |
| **`Q.parochialism` (starts 25)** | Would drive **H+/C−** on first/second wave via `xenophobia_derived` — but stacks only ramp when parochialism **> 41**, so at new game this path is **inactive** |

Third-wave stratum scores exist in the model, but that population is mostly irrelevant until `third_wave_on_society` and migration events fire.

---

## Player-facing mechanics that **do** move polls (non-zero modifiers)

### Deck cards you can actually draw (`#party_affairs` / `#govt_affairs`)

| Card | Choice stacks | Poll impact (examples) |
|------|----------------|-------------------------|
| **Municipal AI** | `ai_open_maintenance`, `ai_contract_audit`, `ai_corp_admin` | Coops **D** +0.12/stack; global **C** −0.08 to +0.1; drones **D** −0.06 on corp-admin path |
| **Media ideology** | `anti_corp_media` only (meaningful) | Global **D** +2 per stack; **rich** **D** −4 per stack |
| **Media ideology** | `self_sufficiency_media`, `kiss_ass_media` | Stacks increment; catalog **delta 0** → **no poll change** |

### Month-end **events** (`#event`)

| Event | Branch stacks | Poll impact |
|-------|---------------|-------------|
| **MSS Dundalk Affair** (Nov 2301) | `dundalk_reform` | Global **D** +1 per stack |
| | `dundalk_stakeholder` | Global **C** −2, **D** +1 per stack |
| | `dundalk_vigil` | Global **G** −2, **D** +0.5 per stack |
| | `dundalk_nationalist` (+20 in one choice) | Global **D** +30 and **H** +30 in one shot (`+1.5` and `+1.5` × 20) |
| **September Convention** (Oct 2300) | `patriot_meme_strength` (permit) | Stack exists; modifier **delta 0** → **no polls** |
| **Abandoned Ballroom** (post-refugee wave) | `ballroom_refugee_housing` | **third_wave** & **alien** **D** +3 per stack; **Docks** **D** −0.5 per stack |
| **Abandoned Ballroom** | Corp / elite grants | Homes/assets only — **no electorate rows** for those branches |
| **Great Protector dispute** (2305+) | `great_protector_appeasement` | Global **C** +0.12, **D** −0.05 per stack (if you reach it) |

### Refugee-wave majors (Feb–Apr 2302)

| Event | Stacks | Poll impact |
|-------|--------|-------------|
| Titan / Earth / Otherworld majors | `jovian_refugee_prepare`, `jovian_refugee_dread` | Modifiers registered with **delta 0** → **no poll change** today |
| Titan | `third_wave_on_society = 1` | Same — tracking flag only |

---

## Implemented in catalog but **not reachable** or **no effect**

| Mechanic | Why it doesn’t affect support in play |
|----------|--------------------------------------|
| **5 organize cards** (education, contracts, coop, magic, militancy) | Not in any deck (`main` has no `#organize_phase`); even if wired, modifiers are wired and would work |
| **Community outreach** (`.old`) | Not compiled |
| **Security** (`police_reform`, `police_defund`, `militia`, …) | Stacks change; all listed modifiers are **delta 0** |
| **Militia degrade** | Changes `militia` / `militia_auxiliary`; **delta 0** |
| **Orc marriage** (Oct–Dec 2301) | Sets `orc_marriage_*`; **delta 0** (old direct `Q.first["Dems"]` edits are commented out) |
| **Cable break**, **Otherworld migrations** card | No poll stacks |
| **`refugee_pressure_hate`, `alien_dems_feb2300`, `pitts_gang_jun2300`** | Catalog entries use legacy IDs as stack names — **`Q[those]` never set** → no effect |
| **September / patriot meme, budget, kiss-ass media, self-sufficiency media** | **delta 0** placeholders |
| **Shuffle cabinet** | Weimar gating (`SPD`, etc.) — won’t show in Mikasa |

---

## Built systems that **don’t feed polls** (yet)

| System | Status |
|--------|--------|
| **`Q.monthly_tick_phase1()`** (prosperity mesh, housing build, `organize_tick` → `protest_good` / `protest_bad`) | Defined in `organize_phase_tick.scene.dry` but **never called** from `advance` / `post_event` |
| **Block `prosperity`** | Updated by tick/assets; **not** in `blockCompetitiveScores()` — prosperity affects displays/crime math, not vote softmax directly |
| **`soylent_anger` / `magrail_anger`** (Dundalk anti-corp branch) | Bumped in JS; **no electorate modifiers** |
| **Jacobs / economic assets** | Housing and assets affect prosperity over time if tick ran; **no direct party modifiers** from advisor actions |
| **`organize_tick` / `non_violence` / `sabotage`** | Would need monthly tick + separate catalog hooks; protest counters aren’t wired to polls |

---

## Practical summary

**In play today**, party support mostly moves from:

1. **Static demography + districts + global_shifts** (the whole table starts biased)
2. **~3 party deck choices** with real modifiers (Municipal AI, anti-corp media)
3. **Dundalk affair** (largest deliberate swing, especially nationalist branch)

## [2026-06-06T20:15:27Z] assistant (afterAgentResponse)

Here’s a reference keyed to **Status** tabs. **Poll impact** means movement in `poll_exec_*`, stratum `*_poll_*`, district `*_poll_*`, or committed seats after elections.

**Legend — Poll impact column**
| Label | Meaning |
|-------|---------|
| **Yes** | Wired through `electorateModifierCatalog` with non-zero delta |
| **Indirect** | Feeds the model but not via story stacks (baselines, IG floors, elections) |
| **Would** | Catalog exists; stacks move in play but delta is 0 or card isn’t drawable |
| **No** | Display / tracking only today |
| **Output** | Computed from the model; you don’t set it directly |

---

## Status → main page

| Shown | Player lever | Poll impact | Notes |
|-------|----------------|-------------|-------|
| **Month / year** | Advance month | **Output** | Triggers `update_projections` + scheduled elections |
| **IG attitudes** (`attitude_*`) | `*_leverage`, `*_reprisal` | **No** | Set at new game; almost nothing changes them in play (`soylent_anger` from Dundalk isn’t wired to attitudes) |
| **Current seats** (`Exec_*`, `Gold_*`, district `*_C`…) | Elections when `elections_this_month` | **Indirect** | Opening seats from Jan 2300 sim; later seats update when `runScheduledElections` commits |
| **Budget** | Dundalk affair branches (+2 each) | **Would** | `budget` has a catalog row with **delta 0**; also fed by housing tax if monthly tick runs (it doesn’t today) |

---

## Status → Politics

| Shown | Player lever | Poll impact | Notes |
|-------|----------------|-------------|-------|
| **Parochialism** | Nothing in play | **Would** | Starts 25; xenophobia modifiers only kick in above **41** via `xenophobia_derived` |
| **Parochial radicalism** | Set at new game (`0.65`) | **Indirect** | Scales how much xenophobia Dems can “claw back” from H |

---

## Status → Economy

| Shown | Player lever | Poll impact | Notes |
|-------|----------------|-------------|-------|
| **Stratum prosperity** (`prosperity_rich`, etc.) | Monthly tick, assets, housing (if tick ran) | **No** | Block prosperity isn’t in vote tabulation; affects crime display on district map |
| **3rd Wave prosperity** | Refugee wave + housing events | **No** | Row hidden until `third_wave_on_society = 1` |
| **Stock prices** (SOYL, MAGR, BRAZ) | Derived from `prosperity_soylent` etc. | **No** | Cosmetic sync from corp prosperity numbers |
| **Syndicate indices** (C9, CIP, LOI) | Derived from `prosperity_families/edges/sharks` | **No** | Same |

---

## Status → Paramilitaries

| Shown | Player lever | Poll impact | Notes |
|-------|----------------|-------------|-------|
| **Police reform** | Security card (improve pay, investigation) | **Would** | Stack moves; catalog **delta 0** |
| **Police defund** | Security → purge ranks | **Would** | Same |
| **Police investigated** | Security → investigation | **Would** | Same |
| **Community militia** | Activist training; militia degrade (−⅓) | **Would** | Same; degrade event is the only post-start change |
| **Militia auxiliary** | Militia degrade → formalize | **Would** | Same |
| **Militia policing** | Security → volunteer militias (gated `corporate_long_leash = TBD`) | **Would** | Choice likely broken; stack would move if picked |

---

## Status → Polls (all output)

| Shown | Player lever | Poll impact | Notes |
|-------|----------------|-------------|-------|
| **Citywide %** (`poll_exec_*`) | Everything below that feeds modifiers | **Output** | Refreshed on tab open |
| **Stratum %** (`rich_poll_*`, etc.) | Same | **Output** | Drones show abstentions, not party % |
| **District %** (`Docks_poll_*`, etc.) | Same | **Output** | District-election model |

---

## Hidden stacks (not on Status, but player-set)

These are the **actual poll levers** today:

| Stack | How you move it | Poll effect (per stack) |
|-------|-----------------|-------------------------|
| `anti_corp_media` | Media ideology → labor focus | Global **D +2**; rich **D −4** |
| `ai_open_maintenance` | Municipal AI → open models | Coop **D +0.12**; global **C −0.08** |
| `ai_contract_audit` | Municipal AI → audit contracts | Global **D +0.1**, **C −0.12** |
| `ai_corp_admin` | Municipal AI → corp clerks | Global **C +0.1**; drone **D −0.06** |
| `dundalk_reform` | Dundalk → justice reform | Global **D +1** |
| `dundalk_stakeholder` | Dundalk → anti-corp rhetoric | Global **C −2**, **D +1** |
| `dundalk_vigil` | Dundalk → anti-crime | Global **G −2**, **D +0.5** |
| `dundalk_nationalist` | Dundalk → offworld anger (+20 once) | Global **D +30**, **H +30** |
| `ballroom_refugee_housing` | Ballroom → refugee housing | 3rd-wave & alien **D +3**; Docks **D −0.5** |
| `great_protector_appeasement` | 2305 dispute (if reached) | Global **C +0.12**, **D −0.05** |

**Stacks you can set but that don’t move polls yet** (delta 0 or unreachable):

`patriot_meme_strength`, `self_sufficiency_media`, `kiss_ass_media`, `jovian_refugee_prepare`, `jovian_refugee_dread`, `orc_marriage_*`, all **security/militia** stacks, all **organize** stacks (`vocational_track`, `contract_*`, `coop_*`, `magic_*`, `non_violence`, `sabotage` — organize cards aren’t in the deck anyway).

---

## Always-on (never shown on Status)

| Mechanic | Poll impact |
|----------|-------------|
| Stratum/district **baseline lean** (coop→D, drones→C, Pitts→G…) | **Indirect** — shapes every projection |
| **`Q.global_shifts`** (Ind −20, Gang −1, Hate −3) | **Indirect** |
| **Interest-group membership floors** | **Indirect** |
| **Scheduled district elections** | **Indirect** — apply current projections to seat counts |

---

**Bottom line for reading Status:** treat **Polls** and **Current seats** as the election model’s output; treat **Politics / Economy / Paramilitaries** as mostly **display or future hooks** except parochialism (inactive until >41). The levers that actually move the Polls tab today are a **small set of hidden stacks** set by Municipal AI, anti-corp media, Dundalk, and (post-2302) ballroom refugee housing — not the numbers on the Politics or Economy tabs.

{
  "file_path": "/home/airy/mikasa/source/scenes/cards/local_organizing/education.scene.dry",
  "content": "title: Education and training\nnew-page: true\nis-card: true\ncard-image: img/asteroid.jpg\ntags: local_organizing, city_affairs\nview_if: organizing_unlock = 1\n\nThe education system has both greatly advanced and greatly regressed since the pre-spaceflight era.  On the positive side, ubiquitous open sourced computerized educational resources have made it trivial for parents to provide children with basic life skills. On the negative side, most children are not recieving formal schooling.  Unschooled children are often left unprepared to the workforce and ignorant of world affairs.  The problem is particularly severe among the non-human populations from the otherworld.\n\n-@too_broke: We dont have the resources right now\n-@vocational\n-@home_ec\n-@well_rounded\n\n@vocational\ntitle: Focus on vocational training to reduce poverty\nchoose-if: 1\non-arrival: vocational_track += 1\n\nImproving vocational training helps workers get better jobs, in particular with the corporations.  School attendence has risen somewhat as students are attracted by the prospect of higher incomes.  The megacorps approve of this curriculum and agree to contract some of their internal training programs over to our education system which allows us to offset the costs of this expansion.\n\n@home_ec\ntitle: Expand teaching of home economics skills like nutrition, health, electronics and mechanics\n\nWe strive to give students the skills to be more self reliant, making their food nuYen go farther, preventing health complications and repairing their home goods instead of buying new ones.  The cooperatives approve of this curriculum and some of their home schooled students have started attending classes part time.\n\n\n@well_rounded\ntitle: Provide a well rounded education\n\nWe are laying the foundation for a true public education system.  This is an expensive choice but will improve education outcomes across the board in the long term.  This is particularly beneficial for the otherworld population.\n\n@too_broke\ntitle: We dont have the resources right now\non-arrival: {! !}"
}

{
  "file_path": "/home/airy/mikasa/source/scenes/cards/local_organizing/peer_media.scene.dry",
  "content": "title: Media ideology \nnew-page: true\nis-card: true\ncard-image: img/asteroid.jpg\ntags: local_organizing\n\nThere are few proper outlets on Mikasa, the corporations shut down any independent competition with their propoganda that is too successful.  A handful of small outlets get by under the radar of the megacorps but most independent journalists rely on annonymous online peer to peer networks to get their reporting out.  Since most members of this small community follow each other's stories, the zeitgeist of the underground media shifts from time to time.\n\nDepending on what topics the underground is most interested in, different groups might become politically active.  What topics is the underground covering?\n\n-@labor\n-@wealthy\n-@diplomatic\n\n@labor\ntitle: Abusive working conditions and the need for worker protections\nsubtitle: Popular among the poor and pisses off nobody but the megacorps\non-arrival: anti_corp_media += 1\n\nPopulist rhetoric is an easy way to get people wanting change.  The Stakeholder revolt might have been crushed but plenty of people are stakeholders in everything but name.\n\n@wealthy\ntitle: stress the need for self sufficiency and protection of businesses\nsubtitle: High skilled workers and cooperatives would like this but the establishment tone would be offputting to workers\nview-if: self_sufficiency_media = 0\non-arrival: self_sufficiency_media += 1\n\nTerrestrials or Martians might be surprised that discussions on IP restrictions on agrinutritional growth mediums can draw an audience but the matter is literally a matter of life and death for many in the cooperatives.  The wealthy usually are hostile towards the cooperatives but the discussion creates some cross-class solidarity, at least among the non-shareholder wealthy.\n\n@diplomatic\ntitle: discussion of Mikasa's place in the solar system economy and negotiations with megacorps\nsubtitle: We might be able to negotiate with corps in good faith, but any economic compromise would undermine self sufficiency\non-arrival: kiss_ass_media += 1\n\nMost people aren't interested in solarpolitical theory crafting but the podcast crowd keeps talking about it.  It doesn't move the needle on public opinion but maybe some good ideas will emerge from it."
}
```

{
  "file_path": "/home/airy/mikasa/source/scenes/cards/local_organizing/migrations.scene.dry",
  "content": "title: Otherworld migrations\nmax-visits: 1\nis-card: true\ncard-image: img/asteroid.jpg\nnew-page: true\ntags: local_organizing\nview-if: month < 6 and year = 2300\n\nMagical passages between Mikasa and the arcane Otherworld have existed since soon after colonization.  The links between Mikasa and the Otherworld are not the most accessible or best travelled in the solar system but the Otherworlder population is proportonally large compared to the small population of Mikasa.  The situation hasn't drawn much notice to date except for the thriving {!<span class=\"tooltip-text\" style=\"color:rgba(149, 139, 0, 1);\" title=\"An non human sentient race from the otherworld, commonly called 'Elves' due to the cultural legacy of 20th century author and Otherworld explorer J.R.R. Tolkein.\">Kwendi</span>!} resturaunt culture in the Docks district.\n\nOtherworld migration has changed of late.  There are a larger number of otherworld travellers and an increase in {!<span class=\"tooltip-text\" style=\"color:rgba(18, 44, 4, 1);\" title=\"A greyish-green skinned sentient race from the otherworld.  They are frequently refered to by the racial slur 'Orcs' due to their close association with the sorcerers of the otherworld and their Orci creations.  To the limited extent that Otherworld biology has been scientifically studied, no link has been found between Uruks and Orci.\">Uruks</span>!}.  Our magical sages tell us the influx is driven by the overall trend towards travel getting easier (the long term cycle of otherworld alignment) and towards a new wave of demonic tyranny in the otherworld.\n\n-@problem: Another crisis to deal with...\n-@opportunity: ...or another opportunity to sieze?\n\n\n@problem\ntitle: When it rains it pours.\n# dendry: was `@nothing The …` on one line (malformed id — prose must follow @subscene header)\n\nThe {!<span class=\"tooltip-text\" style=\"color:rgba(18, 44, 4, 1);\" title=\"A greyish-green skinned sentient race from the otherworld.  They are frequently refered to by the racial slur 'Orcs' due to their close association with the sorcerers of the otherworld and their Orci creations.  To the limited extent that Otherworld biology has been scientifically studied, no link has been found between Uruks and Orci.\">Uruks</span>!} are just one of countless cultures teeming in the depths of Mikasa.  Unlike most of the other's they tend to be illiterate and completely unfamiliar with modern technology.  While it would be humane to help them, they aren't a priority\n\n@opportunity\ntitle: ...or another opportunity to sieze?\n# dendry: was `@opportunity The …` on one line (malformed id)\n\nThe solar system has shown little interest in the otherworld besides in the magical arts of it's people.  An outreach effort could help us be less blind to affairs in the otherworld.  The Uruk migrants in particular appear to have migrated great distances across the otherworld.  A few community centers to teach languages and basic technical literacy go a long way towards winning over the population.  Some do resent the otherworlder's for getting special accomodations when city services are so threadbare."
}

{
  "file_path": "/home/airy/mikasa/source/scenes/cards/city_affairs/municipal_ai.scene.dry",
  "content": "title: Municipal AI\nnew-page: true\nis-card: true\ncard-image: img/asteroid.jpg\ntags: city_affairs\n\n= Municipal AI\n\n# Card by Otto the LLM (Cursor), May 2026.\n\nMikasa was built to run itself. Recyclers, magrails, and gravity plating still sit in the walls—but whole wings stay dark because anything that phones home through the public nets can catch a {!<span class=\"tooltip-text\" title=\"Corrupted AI—botnet code that infects systems on the public nets. The Clans call them Demons.\">Demon</span>!}. The {!<span class=\"tooltip-text\" title=\"Internet relay satellites around Mikasa; plenty of capacity if the nets were clean.\">halo</span>!} has bandwidth to spare. What we have is a {!<span class=\"tooltip-text\" title=\"Self-expanding botnets filled public systems with spam. Too many people profit for anyone in power to fix it.\">dead internet</span>!} instead.\n\nEvery working assistant here is a {!<span class=\"tooltip-text\" title=\"AIs that borrow cycles across the station and move on before they can be corrupted.\">digital migrant</span>!}—borrow cycles, finish the job, disappear. The question is who gets to send them.\n\n- @open_maintenance\n- @contract_audit\n- @corp_clerks\n\n@open_maintenance\ntitle: Deploy open municipal models for grid and life-support\nsubtitle: Air-gapped from the dead internet; popular with cooperatives\non-arrival: ai_open_maintenance += 1\n\nTrain small models on sealed sensor logs and run them on idle municipal hardware, never touching the spam seas. Technicians in the Deeps and Aurora can read the weights. It will not reopen a corridor as fast as a megacorp migrant—but cooperatives would rather patch their own stack than rent another chokepoint.\n\n@contract_audit\ntitle: Audit every corporate AI baked into city contracts\nsubtitle: Test for Demon exposure and dead-internet bleed-through\non-arrival: ai_contract_audit += 1\n\nPayroll, permits, and security cameras increasingly run on vendor models nobody inspects. Publish what they are, demand proof of isolation, and pull contracts when an assistant bleeds into public nets. The corporations will call it sabotage. Anyone who has watched a recycler brick mid-cycle will call it overdue.\n\n@corp_clerks\ntitle: Let megacorp AI clerks handle permits and billing\nsubtitle: Fast migrants that already navigate the dead internet\non-arrival: ai_corp_admin += 1\n\nCommunity Soylent and Magrail Combine already run admin bots that know how to hustle through the mess. Hand them the queue and the wait times collapse. Drone-tier clerks start updating their résumés—and nobody can quite say what else rides in on the connection.\n"
}

{

  "content": "title: Security \nnew-page: true\nis-card: true\ncard-image: img/asteroid.jpg\ntags: city_affairs\nview_if: police_reform < 4\non-arrival: corporate_long_leash = TBD;\n\nCity security historically has earned little faith from the people of Mikasa.  The corporations long used the police as a dumping ground for the cast off of their private security forces.  The poor payment of the police made them easy targets for syndicate bribery.\n\n[? if police_reform = 1: We have taken some steps to clean up the police but have a long way to go ?]\n[? if police_reform = 2: We are reforming the police but there is more to do ?]\n[? if police_defund > 2: We have extensively purged the police of corruption, leaving them short staffed ?]\n\n-@defund\n-@improve_pay\n-@organize_militias\n-@investigation\n\n@defund\ntitle: Purge the ranks\nsubtitle: are corrupt police worse then no police at all?\nchoose-if: police_defund = 0 and police_reform < 2\non-arrival: police_defund = 1\n\nPurging the ranks of the police is a drastic move but ironically one that neither the corporations or syndicates can interfere with.  This frees up budget which could hopefully go towards making less corrupt institutions.  \n[? if militia_police = 1: Hopefully our militia forces can prevent the streets decending into chaos ?]\n[? if militia_police = 0: We better have a plan to prevent anarchy in the streets ?]\n\n@improve_pay\ntitle: Improve pay and training\non-arrival: police_reform += 1; police_reform += 1 if militia_police > 1; police_reform += 1 if police_defund=1\n# dendry: on-depart is not a valid property (was: on-depart: defund = 0)\n\nPaying the police more then a poverty wage can make them less dependent on bribes.\n[? if police_defund = 1 and militia_police < 2 : We can hire more honest replacements for the police we previously purged. ?]\n[? if militia_police > 1: The local militia's serve as a recruiting pool for non corrupt security officers. ?]\n\n@organize_militias\ntitle: Organize volunteer community militias to take over police duties\nchoose-if: corporate_long_leash = 1\nunavailable-subtitle: [? if corporate_long_leash = 0 : The megacorps dont trust us enough to allow this. ?][? if miltia >= 2: There are limits to what volunteers can accomplish ?]\non-arrival: megacorps_angry_TBD += 1; militia_police += 1\n\nWe have established volunteer neighborhood protection services.  This is an alternative to the police but we should be careful about relying on them indefinitely, over time their strength will wane and they could turn into protection rackets themselves.\n\n@investigation\ntitle: Start a discrete investigation\nsubtitle: Who watches the watchman?\nchoose-if: police_investigated = 0\non-arrival: police_reform += 1; police_reform +=1 if police_reform>1; megacorps_angry_TBD += 1 if police_reform>1;  police_investigated=1\n\n[? if police_reform = 1: Finding corrupt police is like shooting fish in a barrel.  Our investigation unit makes dozens of arrest before word gets out and the corrupt officials get better at covering their tracks. ?]\n\n[? if police_reform > 1: Two gunshots ring out from a highrise appartment in the docks district in the early hours of the morning.  Two bodies go flying through the window, showering the street below in glass.  One figure clings precariously to a piece of signage, the other grabs him by the neck.  \"Stop you fool, we'll both die!\" the choked man sputters.  \"Then I'll see you in hell.\" says the trenchcoated investigator before finishing the job. ?]"

{
  "file_path": "/home/airy/mikasa/source/scenes/cards/local_organizing/community_outreach.scene.dry",
  "content": "title: Community Conflicts\nnew-page: true\nis-card: true\ncard-image: img/asteroid.jpg\ntags: local_organizing\n\n= Community Conflicts\n\n- @first_wave_community\n- @second_wave_community\n- @isolated_communities\n- @drones_community\n- @otherworlders_community\n\n@first_wave_community\ntitle: Calm the first wave settlers\nsubtitle: Japanese, European, American\non-arrival: first_wave_dissent -= 10; first_wave_strength += 1;\n\nThe first settlers were Japanese, whose numbers were joined by European and American colonists.  They are some of the best established and most prosperous people of Mikasa but the rivalries created by the chaos on earth leads to resentments in space, both between each other and towards the more recent arrivals.\n\nBy mediating and addressing private disputes and small conflicts, we have reduced unrest in this community.\n\n\n@second_wave_community\ntitle: Stabilize second wave communities\nsubtitle: Bantu, Middle Eastern, Southeastern Asian\non-arrival: second_wave_prosperity += 1; second_wave_dissent -= 5;\n\nMikasa and the other transsolar waystations became refugee destinations as the conflicts on Earth and the Jovian Moons worsened.  Many of the refugees came from places that had no previous ties to Mikasa.  The corporations were eager to have them come to serve as cheap labor but unconcerned with sustainable living conditions.  Uprooted from their existing support networks they have struggled but over time new communities are emerging.\n\nAddressing shortfalls in Mikasa's infrastructure and making connections with local community leaders helps address the poor living conditions and conflicts among these groups.\n\n@isolated_communities\ntitle: Outreach to isolated communities\nsubtitle: Deepers and Tunnel Clans\non-arrival: isolated_dissent -= 5;\n\nThe deepers are a community that originally settled on a different site of the asteroid from the tether ground station.  They intensely resent their annexation and desire a return to autonomy.  The tunnel clans are communities that left the artificial gravity grid and hid themselves away deep in the embrace of the \"mother\".  Their technical savviness could be an asset if we could overcome their isolation but this will be difficult as they can take care of their own and have little need for us.\n\n@drones_community\ntitle: Make inroads among the corporate employees\nsubtitle: Hereditary worker caste\non-arrival: drones_dissent -= 5;\n\nMost corporate employees on Mikasa work for the Big Three, Community Soylent, Magrail Combine and Brazos Universal.  Corporate employeeship has become a hereditary caste that jealously guards their privledges and relative wealth over the underclass.\n\nAny efforts to reach out to this group will inevitably be called illegal labor organizing.  Still there are more daring employees who have learned to keep things off the bosses radar.\n\n\n@otherworlders_community\ntitle: Assimilate arrivals from the otherworld\nsubtitle: Ruukish (otherworld humans), Fae, Orcs\non-arrival: otherworld_assimilation += 1;\n\nAlthough small in number, the refugees from the mystic otherworld have extreme culture shock.  What's worse they have brought a bitter ethnic conflict with them, the Orci question.  The orcs are often conflated with the orci, magical immitations of life while the fae are accused of ensnaring others in their magics.  The otherworld humans who typically are at the bottom of the heirarchy in the otherworld resent both groups but have trouble adjusting to a technological existence when they rarely possess basic literacy.\n\nWe can help educate the otherworlders in our ways, hopefullly learning from the times humanity has put aside it's conflicts instead of allowing them to fester.  And in turn we might learn more of the magical arts of the otherworld.  Even if few of the refugees were trained in the magical arts, they have seen far more magic then the vast majority of people from this world.\n"
}
```

{
  "file_path": "/home/airy/mikasa/source/scenes/cards/local_organizing/prosperity_connection_catalog.md",
  "content": "# Prosperity connection catalog\n\nImplemented in `source/scenes/cards/local_organizing/organize_phase_tick.scene.dry` (search `MIKASA_ORGANIZE_PHASE_TICK` / `PROSPERITY_*`). Monthly tick applies **legacy** `Q.prosperityConnectionCatalog` (decay only), then district mesh, IG home links, Docks trade, Pitts crime. Housing and destroyed-home prosperity: `source/scenes/economic_assets_runtime.scene.dry`.\n\nCLI tune check: `npm run simulate-prosperity` (`scripts/simulate-prosperity-months.js`).\n\nTransfer scale (all structural links): `coeff × signal × 2·sourcePop / (sourcePop + targetPop)`.\n\n- **Block → block:** `sourcePop` = source block population.\n- **IG → block:** `sourcePop` = `PROSPERITY_IG_EFFECTIVE_POP[ig]`.\n- **Quality → block:** `sourcePop` = total district population.\n\nMissing matrix entry or `0` coefficient = wired but no transfer.\n\n## 1. Within-district mesh (`PROSPERITY_INTRA_DISTRICT_DEFAULT`)\n\nSame sparse matrix in every district. Only nonzero pairs listed; all other directed pairs = 0.\n\n| From → To | rich | first | second | coop | drone | alien | deeper | 3rd |\n|-----------|------|-------|--------|------|-------|-------|--------|-----|\n| rich | — | 0.0005 | 0.0015 | 0.0004 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 |\n| first | 0.0003 | — | 0.002 | 0.001 | 0.0004 | 0 | 0 | 0 |\n| second | 0.0004 | 0.002 | — | 0.0012 | 0.0005 | 0 | 0 | 0 |\n| coop | 0.0003 | 0.001 | 0.001 | — | 0.0006 | 0 | 0 | 0 |\n| drone | 0 | 0.0003 | 0.0004 | 0.0008 | — | 0 | 0 | 0 |\n| alien | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | — | 0.0004 | 0 |\n| deeper | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0.0004 | — | 0 |\n| third_wave | 0 | 0.0004 | 0.0006 | 0.0004 | 0 | 0 | 0 | — |\n\nSignal = source block prosperity (level). Override per district via `Q.prosperityIntraDistrictMesh[districtId]`.\n\n## 2. IG home district (`PROSPERITY_IG_HOME_DISTRICT_LINKS`)\n\n| IG | District | Highlights (0 = explicit no spill) |\n|----|----------|-------------------------------------|\n| Soylent | Vats | drone 0.009; coop/second 0.002–0.0015; alien 0 |\n| Magrail | Railyard | drone 0.008; coop 0.0035 |\n| Brazos | Limelight | drone 0.008; first 0.004 |\n| Families | Pitts | second 0.004; rich/drone 0 |\n| Edges | Railyard | second 0.003; drone 0.0025 |\n| Sharks | Deeps | deeper 0.005; alien 0.003; drone 0 |\n\nSignal = IG `prosperity_*` (level). All eight strata listed per row where blocks exist.\n\n## 3. Docks trade (`PROSPERITY_DOCKS_TRADE`)\n\n- `trade_level` excess → **every** Docks block.\n- Base coeff **0.01** × strata multiplier: rich 1.2, first/second 1, coop 0.9, drone 0.8, alien/deeper 0.7, third_wave 0.8.\n\n## 4. Pitts crime (`PROSPERITY_PITTS_CRIME`)\n\n- `crime_level` excess → **first, second, third_wave, coop, deeper, alien** only (not rich, not drone).\n- Base coeff **−0.012** (strata entry `1` = full weight).\n\n## Legacy catalog\n\nStratum/IG self-decay rows unchanged. Removed citywide corp→stratum, syndicate→district, and old uniform trade/crime district rows (replaced above).\n\n## Tuning hooks on `Q`\n\n- `prosperityIntraDistrictMesh` / `prosperityIntraDistrictMeshDefault`\n- `prosperityIgHomeDistrictLinks`\n- `prosperityDocksTrade` / `prosperityPittsCrime`\n- `prosperityIgEffectivePop`\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/home/airy/mikasa/source/scenes/electorate_runtime.scene.dry",
  "content": "title: Electorate runtime\nmax-visits: 1\nnew-page: false\non-arrival: {!\n\n// MIKASA_ELECTORATE_RUNTIME_BEGIN\n// --- Electorate modifiers (applied after vote-label sync in Q.update_projections) ---\n// stackId: Q quality bumped in events (e.g. favor_second_wave_housing_dispute += 1).\n// Several rows may share one stackId (multiple effects per choice).\n// Dendry initializes missing qualities to 0 before +=. Each poll applies delta * stacks.\n// Scopes: stratum (demographic), district (region), global.\n\nvar ELECTORATE_PARTY_KEYS = ['D', 'I', 'C', 'G', 'H']\n\nfunction electorateZeroScores() {\n    return { D: 0, I: 0, C: 0, G: 0, H: 0 }\n}\n\nfunction electorateCopyScores(src) {\n    var out = electorateZeroScores()\n    for (var i = 0; i < ELECTORATE_PARTY_KEYS.length; i++) {\n        var k = ELECTORATE_PARTY_KEYS[i]\n        out[k] = (src && src[k]) || 0\n    }\n    return out\n}\n\nfunction electorateAddDelta(scores, party, delta) {\n    if (scores && scores[party] !== undefined) {\n        scores[party] += delta\n    }\n}\n\nvar ELECTORATE_SCOPES = { stratum: 1, district: 1, global: 1 }\n\nclass ElectorateModifier {\n    /**\n     * Positional (table-friendly):\n     *   new ElectorateModifier(rowId, stackId, scope, target, party, delta [, activeIf])\n     *   new ElectorateModifier(stackId, scope, target, party, delta [, activeIf])  // legacy: rowId = stackId\n     * stackId: Q quality for stacks (favor_second_wave_housing_dispute += 1).\n     * scope: 'stratum' | 'district' | 'global' — use '' for target when global.\n     * Object form: { id, stackId?, scope, target, party, delta, activeIf? }\n     */\n    constructor(rowId, stackId, scope, target, party, delta, activeIf) {\n        var fromObject = rowId && typeof rowId === 'object'\n        if (fromObject) {\n            var opts = rowId\n            rowId = opts.id\n            stackId = opts.stackId != null ? opts.stackId : opts.id\n            scope = opts.scope\n            target = opts.target\n            party = opts.party\n            delta = opts.delta\n            activeIf = opts.activeIf\n            this.gateWithActiveIf = activeIf != null\n        } else if (ELECTORATE_SCOPES[stackId]) {\n            // legacy: (stackId, scope, target, party, delta [, activeIf])\n            activeIf = delta\n            delta = party\n            party = target\n            target = scope\n            scope = stackId\n            stackId = rowId\n            this.gateWithActiveIf = arguments.length >= 6 && activeIf != null\n        } else {\n            this.gateWithActiveIf = arguments.length >= 7 && activeIf != null\n        }\n        this.id = rowId\n        this.stackId = stackId || rowId\n        this.scope = scope\n        this.target = target || ''\n        this.party = party\n        this.delta = delta || 0\n        this.activeIf = activeIf || function() { return true }\n    }\n\n    /** Stack count from Q[stackId]; non-numeric or <= 0 → no effect. */\n    stacksFromQ(q) {\n        if (!q || q[this.stackId] == null) { return 0 }\n        var n = Number(q[this.stackId])\n        if (isNaN(n) || n <= 0) { return 0 }\n        return n\n    }\n\n    isActive(q) {\n        try {\n            return !!this.activeIf(q)\n        } catch (e) {\n            return false\n        }\n    }\n\n    apply(state, q) {\n        var stacks = this.stacksFromQ(q)\n        if (stacks <= 0) { return }\n        if (this.gateWithActiveIf && !this.isActive(q)) { return }\n        var shift = this.delta * stacks\n        if (this.scope === 'stratum') {\n            var dem = state.demographicsById[this.target]\n            if (dem) { electorateAddDelta(dem.competitiveScores, this.party, shift) }\n        } else if (this.scope === 'district') {\n            var dist = state.districtsById[this.target]\n            if (dist) { electorateAddDelta(dist.competitiveScores, this.party, shift) }\n        } else if (this.scope === 'global') {\n            electorateAddDelta(state.global.competitiveScores, this.party, shift)\n        }\n    }\n}\n\nQ.ElectorateModifier = ElectorateModifier\n\n// rowId | stackId (= Q) | scope | ElectorateModifier('_ref_Deeps_I', 'district', 'Deeps', 'I', 0.4),\n// new ElectorateModifier('_ref_Deeps_H', 'district', 'Deeps', 'H', -0.3),\n\n/** How radical the xenophobia is (0–1 linear, then sqrt above 1). Higher → harder for Dems to claw back. */\nfunction effectiveParochialRadicalism(rad) {\n    if (isNaN(rad) || rad <= 0) { return 0 }\n    if (rad <= 1) { return rad }\n    return Math.sqrt(rad)\n}\n\n/** Share of derived xenophobia Dems can claw off H (1 − radicalism, floored at 0). */\nfunction demClawBackFraction(rad) {\n    return Math.max(0, 1 - effectiveParochialRadicalism(rad))\n}\n\n/**\n * Derive modifier stacks from Q.parochialism (0–100).\n * xenophobia_derived: ramps above Suspicion (41+). H+/C− on 1st/2nd via catalog.\n * xenophobia_clawed_back: derived * demClawBackFraction(rad); C still hit by derived only.\n */\nQ.syncParochialismXenophobiaStacks = function(q) {\n    q = q || Q\n    var p = Number(q.parochialism)\n    if (isNaN(p)) { p = 0 }\n    var rad = Number(q.parochial_radicalism)\n    if (isNaN(rad)) { rad = 0.65 }\n    var clawMult = demClawBackFraction(rad)\n\n    var suspicionFloor = 41\n    var maxStack = 20\n    var raw = p <= suspicionFloor ? 0 : ((p - suspicionFloor) / (100 - suspicionFloor)) * maxStack\n\n    q.xenophobia_derived = raw\n    q.xenophobia_clawed_back = raw * clawMult\n}\n\nQ.applyElectorateModifiers = function() {\n    var state = Q.electionV2\n    if (!state) { return }\n\n    Q.syncParochialismXenophobiaStacks(Q)\n\n    state.demographicsById = state.demographicsById || Object.fromEntries(\n        state.demographics.map(function(d) { return [d.id, d] })\n    )\n    state.districtsById = state.districtsById || Object.fromEntries(\n        state.districts.map(function(d) { return [d.id, d] })\n    )\n\n    // Districts are not rebuilt by syncAllVoteAppeals; reset from baseline each poll.\n    for (var j = 0; j < state.districts.length; j++) {\n        var dist = state.districts[j]\n        dist.competitiveScores = electorateCopyScores(\n            dist._baseCompetitiveScores || dist.competitiveScores\n        )\n    }\n    // Strata/global already base+appeals from election runtime sync; add stack deltas only.\n\n    for (var m = 0; m < Q.electorateModifierCatalog.length; m++) {\n        Q.electorateModifierCatalog[m].apply(state, Q)\n    }\n}\n// MIKASA_ELECTORATE_RUNTIME_END\n\nelectorate_runtime_ready = 1\n\n!}\ngo-to: jumpScene\n"
}

{
  "file_path": "/home/airy/mikasa/source/lib/election-v2.md",
  "content": "# Mikasa election model (v2)\n\nHow council seats are simulated, projected, and committed in play.\n\n## Source of truth (important)\n\n**Edit `source/scenes/**/*.scene.dry`, not `source/lib/*.js`, for anything that affects the playable game.**\n\n| What | Authoritative file |\n|------|-------------------|\n| Election engine, `DISTRICT_POPULATIONS`, `DISTRICT_ELECTION_MONTH`, `Q.update_projections`, `Q.runScheduledElections` | `source/scenes/election_runtime.scene.dry` |\n| Electorate stack → poll modifiers | `source/scenes/electorate_runtime.scene.dry` |\n| Month tick / prosperity connections | `source/scenes/cards/local_organizing/organize_phase_tick.scene.dry` |\n| Housing stock, tax, economic holdings report | `source/scenes/economic_assets_runtime.scene.dry` |\n| Election-night UI | `source/scenes/events/scheduled_elections.scene.dry`, `status.scene.dry` |\n\nDev-only mirrors (keep in sync **after** you change the scene, if you use CLI sims):\n\n- `source/lib/election-v2.js` — legacy parallel implementation; prefer `npm run simulate-election:v2 -- --from-scene` to test compiled scene JS.\n- `source/lib/election-world-data.js` — copy of `DISTRICT_POPULATIONS` / `IG_MEMBERSHIP_BY_STRATUM` from `election_runtime.scene.dry` for `require()` in Node without Dendrynexus.\n\nDisplay of election-night results: compute in scene JS, show with `[+ quality +]` in `.dry` prose (see `scheduled_elections.scene.dry`, `election_simulation.scene.dry`).\n\n---\n\n## Overview\n\nFive parties (keys **D, I, C, G, H**): Dems, Independent, Corp, Gang, Hate.\n\nThree chambers:\n\n| Chamber | Seats | Held on `Q` as | Notes |\n|--------|------:|----------------|--------|\n| Executive | 150 | `Exec_C`, `Exec_D`, … | Full re-election in **December** (`EXEC_ELECTION_MONTH = 12`) |\n| Guild (×3) | 35 each | `Gold_C`, `White_C`, `Blue_C`, … | **Gold / White / Blue** delegations; ¼ seat turnover **every month**, silent (no event card) |\n| District councils (×7) | 15 each | `Docks_C`, `Aurora_D`, … | One district per calendar month; see `DISTRICT_ELECTION_MONTH` in `election_runtime.scene.dry` |\n\n**Projections** use the `Proj_` prefix (e.g. `Proj_Docks_C`). **Committed** seats omit `Proj_`. Status → Politics calls `Q.update_projections()` and reads both.\n\n### Vote model (short)\n\n1. **Voter blocks** = demographic stratum × district (population weights from `DISTRICT_POPULATIONS`).\n2. **Interest groups** assign a fixed “floor” of ballots (corps, syndicates, guild blocs).\n3. **Competitive softmax** on the remaining electorate (turnout varies by district/stratum).\n4. **`assign_percentages`** turns vote totals into integer seats (largest remainder).\n\nElectorate shifts from story cards: stacks on `Q` → `Q.electorateModifierCatalog` in **`electorate_runtime.scene.dry`** (applied inside election tabulation).\n\n---\n\n## File map\n\n| File | Role |\n|------|------|\n| `source/scenes/election_runtime.scene.dry` | **Playable** election engine |\n| `source/scenes/electorate_runtime.scene.dry` | **Playable** modifier catalog |\n| `source/scenes/post_event.scene.dry` | After each month: projections + scheduled elections |\n| `source/scenes/events/scheduled_elections.scene.dry` | Event card when `elections_this_month = 1` |\n| `source/scenes/status.scene.dry` | Poll / seat UI |\n| `source/scenes/election_simulation.scene.dry` | Debug special scene (population table) |\n| `source/scenes/economic_assets_runtime.scene.dry` | Homes inventory, housing tax, Jacobs holdings report |\n| `source/scenes/cards/organizing/organize_phase_tick.scene.dry` | Monthly prosperity connections + tick orchestration |\n| `source/lib/election-v2.js` | Node CLI only — not compiled into the game |\n| `source/lib/election-world-data.js` | Node copy of district populations — not compiled into the game |\n\n---\n\n## Monthly flow\n\n```\nadvance month\n  → post_event (on-arrival)\n       Q.update_projections()      // Proj_* + poll displays; no seat commit\n       Q.runScheduledElections()   // commit seats + maybe fire election event\n  → #event queue (scheduled_elections if elections_this_month = 1)\n  → main\n```\n\n### `Q.update_projections(opts)`\n\nRuns tabulation with **`commitSeats: false`**. Refreshes projections and status polls only.\n\n### `Q.runScheduledElections(opts)`\n\nRuns after projections. Once per month/year unless `opts.force` or `Q.elections_allow_same_month_repeat`.\n\n1. Tabulate current month (`allDistricts: false` for district chamber when appropriate).\n2. **Guild:** `rotateGuildSeatsQuarter` on Gold / White / Blue (silent).\n3. **Executive:** if `month === 12`, commit `Exec_*`.\n4. **Districts:** for each district whose election month matches, commit `District_*` and `publishDistrictElectionReport`.\n5. If any executive or district election ran: `elections_this_month = 1`; else clear.\n\n---\n\n## District election calendar\n\nDefined in **`DISTRICT_ELECTION_MONTH`** inside **`election_runtime.scene.dry`** (if you maintain `election-world-data.js`, update it to match):\n\n| District | Month |\n|----------|------:|\n| Vats | 2 |\n| Aurora | 4 |\n| Pitts | 5 |\n| Docks | 6 |\n| Limelight | 6 |\n| Deeps | 8 |\n| Railyard | 11 |\n\nExecutive: month **12**.\n\n---\n\n## Changing the model\n\n1. Edit **`election_runtime.scene.dry`** (search `MIKASA_ELECTION_RUNTIME_BEGIN`).\n2. If you changed `DISTRICT_POPULATIONS` or `IG_MEMBERSHIP_BY_STRATUM`, mirror the same numbers into **`election-world-data.js`** for CLI tools.\n3. `npm run lint:dendry` then `npm run make-html`.\n4.   "content": "# Mikasa election model (v2)\n\nHow council seats are simulated, projected, and committed in play.\n\n## Source of truth (important)\n\n**Edit `source/scenes/**/*.scene.dry`, not `source/lib/*.js`, for anything that affects the playable game.**\n\n| What | Authoritative file |\n|------|-------------------|\n| Election engine, `DISTRICT_POPULATIONS`, `DISTRICT_ELECTION_MONTH`, `Q.update_projections`, `Q.runScheduledElections` | `source/scenes/election_runtime.scene.dry` |\n| Electorate stack → poll modifiers | `source/scenes/electorate_runtime.scene.dry` |\n| Month tick / prosperity connections | `source/scenes/cards/local_organizing/organize_phase_tick.scene.dry` |\n| Housing stock, tax, economic holdings report | `source/scenes/economic_assets_runtime.scene.dry` |\n| Election-night UI | `source/scenes/events/scheduled_elections.scene.dry`, `status.scene.dry` |\n\nDev-only mirrors (keep in sync **after** you change the scene, if you use CLI sims):\n\n- `source/lib/election-v2.js` — legacy parallel implementation; prefer `npm run simulate-election:v2 -- --from-scene` to test compiled scene JS.\n- `source/lib/election-world-data.js` — copy of `DISTRICT_POPULATIONS` / `IG_MEMBERSHIP_BY_STRATUM` from `election_runtime.scene.dry` for `require()` in Node without Dendrynexus.\n\nDisplay of election-night results: compute in scene JS, show with `[+ quality +]` in `.dry` prose (see `scheduled_elections.scene.dry`, `election_simulation.scene.dry`).\n\n---\n\n## Overview\n\nFive parties (keys **D, I, C, G, H**): Dems, Independent, Corp, Gang, Hate.\n\nThree chambers:\n\n| Chamber | Seats | Held on `Q` as | Notes |\n|--------|------:|----------------|--------|\n| Executive | 150 | `Exec_C`, `Exec_D`, … | Full re-election in **December** (`EXEC_ELECTION_MONTH = 12`) |\n| Guild (×3) | 35 each | `Gold_C`, `White_C`, `Blue_C`, … | **Gold / White / Blue** delegations; ¼ seat turnover **every month**, silent (no event card) |\n| District councils (×7) | 15 each | `Docks_C`, `Aurora_D`, … | One district per calendar month; see `DISTRICT_ELECTION_MONTH` in `election_runtime.scene.dry` |\n\n**Projections** use the `Proj_` prefix (e.g. `Proj_Docks_C`). **Committed** seats omit `Proj_`. Status → Politics calls `Q.update_projections()` and reads both.\n\n### Vote model (short)\n\n1. **Voter blocks** = demographic stratum × district (population weights from `DISTRICT_POPULATIONS`).\n2. **Interest groups** assign a fixed “floor” of ballots (corps, syndicates, guild blocs).\n3. **Competitive softmax** on the remaining electorate (turnout varies by district/stratum).\n4. **`assign_percentages`** turns vote totals into integer seats (largest remainder).\n\nElectorate shifts from story cards: stacks on `Q` → `Q.electorateModifierCatalog` in **`electorate_runtime.scene.dry`** (applied inside election tabulation).\n\n---\n\n## File map\n\n| File | Role |\n|------|------|\n| `source/scenes/election_runtime.scene.dry` | **Playable** election engine |\n| `source/scenes/electorate_runtime.scene.dry` | **Playable** modifier catalog |\n| `source/scenes/post_event.scene.dry` | After each month: projections + scheduled elections |\n| `source/scenes/events/scheduled_elections.scene.dry` | Event card when `elections_this_month = 1` |\n| `source/scenes/status.scene.dry` | Poll / seat UI |\n| `source/scenes/election_simulation.scene.dry` | Debug special scene (population table) |\n| `source/scenes/economic_assets_runtime.scene.dry` | Homes inventory, housing tax, Jacobs holdings report |\n| `source/scenes/cards/local_organizing/organize_phase_tick.scene.dry` | Monthly prosperity connections + tick orchestration |\n| `source/lib/election-v2.js` | Node CLI only — not compiled into the game |\n| `source/lib/election-world-data.js` | Node copy of district populations — not compiled into the game |\n\n---\n\n## Monthly flow\n\n```\nadvance month\n  → post_event (on-arrival)\n       Q.update_projections()      // Proj_* + poll displays; no seat commit\n       Q.runScheduledElections()   // commit seats + maybe fire election event\n  → #event queue (scheduled_elections if elections_this_month = 1)\n  → main\n```\n\n### `Q.update_projections(opts)`\n\nRuns tabulation with **`commitSeats: false`**. Refreshes projections and status polls only.\n\n### `Q.runScheduledElections(opts)`\n\nRuns after projections. Once per month/year unless `opts.force` or `Q.elections_allow_same_month_repeat`.\n\n1. Tabulate current month (`allDistricts: false` for district chamber when appropriate).\n2. **Guild:** `rotateGuildSeatsQuarter` on Gold / White / Blue (silent).\n3. **Executive:** if `month === 12`, commit `Exec_*`.\n4. **Districts:** for each district whose election month matches, commit `District_*` and `publishDistrictElectionReport`.\n5. If any executive or district election ran: `elections_this_month = 1`; else clear.\n\n---\n\n## District election calendar\n\nDefined in **`DISTRICT_ELECTION_MONTH`** inside **`election_runtime.scene.dry`** (if you maintain `election-world-data.js`, update it to match):\n\n| District | Month |\n|----------|------:|\n| Vats | 2 |\n| Aurora | 4 |\n| Pitts | 5 |\n| Docks | 6 |\n| Limelight | 6 |\n| Deeps | 8 |\n| Railyard | 11 |\n\nExecutive: month **12**.\n\n---\n\n## Changing the model\n\n1. Edit **`election_runtime.scene.dry`** (search `MIKASA_ELECTION_RUNTIME_BEGIN`).\n2. If you changed `DISTRICT_POPULATIONS` or `IG_MEMBERSHIP_BY_STRATUM`, mirror the same numbers into **`election-world-data.js`** for CLI tools.\n3. `npm run lint:dendry` then `npm run make-html`.\n4. Optional CLI: `npm run simulate-election:v2 -- --from-scene` (elections), `npm run simulate-prosperity` (prosperity + housing tick)\n\n### New district election month\n\nUpdate `DISTRICT_ELECTION_MONTH` in **`election_runtime.scene.dry`**, mirror to `election-world-data.js` / `election-v2.js` only if you still use the standalone CLI module, and add a conditional table block in `scheduled_elections.scene.dry`.\n\n---\n\n## Credits\n\n- In-game scheduling and Mikasa-specific chambers: Mikasa project.\n- Results UI pattern: **origin0**, `election_1928.scene.dry` (*dynamic_social_democracy*).\n"
}

## [2026-06-06T20:41:40Z] assistant_thought (afterAgentThought)

Adding `bumpDistrictProsperity` to `organize_phase_tick` and the organize tick API bootstrap.

Reconsidering the bootstrap approach. Cards cannot run root-level on-arrival logic, so the simpler path is to inline the prosperity and party support modifiers directly into each subscene's JavaScript. This avoids touching the organize tick API entirely.

The inline code targets the Docks district block, clamps prosperity between 0 and 20, and triggers the required UI refresh functions. Repeating this across eleven subscenes would be verbose.

Settled on adding `Q.bumpDistrictProsperity` to the organize tick API bootstrap instead of inlining code into each subscene. The bootstrap will register `applyStratumProsperityBumps` and `bumpDistrictProsperity` under `ORGANIZE_API`, then route to `organize_phase_tick` when needed. Wealthy strata will receive a 0.04 prosperity bump via `applyStratumProsperityBumps`. The old `contract_big3_drone_C` modifier will be removed. Now writing the full `contracting.scene.dry` and electorate modifier files.

Keeping `contract_outside_global_C` while removing `contract_petty_first_I`, `contract_petty_first_G`, and `contract_coop_coop_D`.

New modifiers are being assigned delta values. `contract_big3_docks` adds +0.12 to Docks, -0.08 to global, and +0.08 to drone stratum. `contract_big3_vats` adds +0.1 to Docks, +0.2 to global, and +0.05 to global from favored corporation. `contract_big3_railyard` adds +0.1 to Docks and +0.2 to global. `contract_big3_limelight` is next.

   fromScope = opts.fromScope\n            fromTarget = opts.fromTarget\n            toScope = opts.toScope\n            toTarget = opts.toTarget\n            coefficient = opts.coefficient\n        } else if (typeof opts !== 'object' || opts === null) {\n            opts = {}\n        }\n        this.id = rowId\n        this.fromScope = fromScope\n        this.fromTarget = fromTarget || ''\n        this.toScope = toScope\n        this.toTarget = toTarget || ''\n        this.coefficient = coefficient || 0\n        this.basis = opts.basis || 'level'\n        this.self_feedback = !!(opts.self_feedback || opts.local)\n        this.stackId = opts.stackId || null\n        this.activeIf = opts.activeIf || function() { return true }\n    }\n\n    stacksFromQ(q) {\n        if (!this.stackId) { return 1 }\n        if (!q || q[this.stackId] == null) { return 0 }\n        var n = Number(q[this.stackId])\n        if (isNaN(n) || n <= 0) { return 0 }\n        return n\n    }\n\n    isActive(q) {\n        try {\n            return !!this.activeIf(q)\n        } catch (e) {\n            return false\n        }\n    }\n\n    basisAmount(value) {\n        var v = Number(value)\n        if (isNaN(v)) { v = defaultProsperityForConnection(this) }\n        if (this.basis === 'excess') {\n            if (this.fromScope === 'quality') {\n                return v - 1\n            }\n            return v - PROSPERITY_BLOCK_BASELINE\n        }\n        return v\n    }\n\n    igProsperityKey(igId) {\n        return 'prosperity_' + String(igId).toLowerCase()\n    }\n\n    blocksMatching(state, scope, target) {\n        if (!state || !state.blocks) { return [] }\n        if (scope === 'block') {\n            return state.blocks.filter(function(b) { return b.id === target })\n        }\n        if (scope === 'stratum') {\n            return state.blocks.filter(function(b) { return b.demographic.id === target })\n        }\n        if (scope === 'district') {\n            return state.blocks.filter(function(b) { return b.district.id === target })\n        }\n        return []\n    }\n\n    weightedBlockProsperity(blocks) {\n        var popSum = 0\n        var weighted = 0\n        for (var i = 0; i < blocks.length; i++) {\n            var b = blocks[i]\n            var pop = b.population\n            if (pop <= 0) { continue }\n            popSum += pop\n            weighted += pop * (b.prosperity ?? PROSPERITY_BLOCK_BASELINE)\n        }\n        return popSum > 0 ? weighted / popSum : PROSPERITY_BLOCK_BASELINE\n    }\n\n    readBroadcastSource(state, q) {\n        if (this.fromScope === 'quality') {\n            var raw = Number(q[this.fromTarget])\n            if (isNaN(raw)) { raw = 1 }\n            return this.basisAmount(raw)\n        }\n        if (this.fromScope === 'ig') {\n            var key = this.igProsperityKey(this.fromTarget)\n            var igVal = q[key]\n            if (igVal == null) { igVal = igProsperityDefault(this.fromTarget) }\n            return this.basisAmount(igVal)\n        }\n        var blocks = this.blocksMatching(state, this.fromScope, this.fromTarget)\n        return this.basisAmount(this.weightedBlockProsperity(blocks))\n    }\n\n    accumulate(state, q, blockDeltas, igDeltas) {\n        if (!this.isActive(q)) { return }\n        var mult = this.stacksFromQ(q)\n        if (mult <= 0) { return }\n\n        if (this.self_feedback) {\n            this.accumulateSelfFeedback(state, q, blockDeltas, igDeltas, mult)\n            return\n        }\n\n        var sourceSignal = this.readBroadcastSource(state, q)\n        var transfer = this.coefficient * sourceSignal * mult\n        if (transfer === 0) { return }\n\n        if (this.toScope === 'ig') {\n            var igKey = this.igProsperityKey(this.toTarget)\n            igDeltas[igKey] = (igDeltas[igKey] || 0) + transfer\n            return\n        }\n\n        var targets = this.blocksMatching(state, this.toScope, this.toTarget)\n        for (var t = 0; t < targets.length; t++) {\n            var id = targets[t].id\n            blockDeltas[id] = (blockDeltas[id] || 0) + transfer\n        }\n    }\n\n    accumulateSelfFeedback(state, q, blockDeltas, igDeltas, mult) {\n        if (this.toScope === 'ig') {\n            var igKey = this.igProsperityKey(this.toTarget)\n            var igVal = q[igKey]\n            if (igVal == null) { igVal = igProsperityDefault(this.toTarget) }\n            var igTransfer = this.coefficient * this.basisAmount(igVal) * mult\n            if (igTransfer !== 0) {\n                igDeltas[igKey] = (igDeltas[igKey] || 0) + igTransfer\n            }\n            return\n        }\n\n        var targets = this.blocksMatching(state, this.toScope, this.toTarget)\n        for (var t = 0; t < targets.length; t++) {\n            var block = targets[t]\n            var fromBlocks = this.blocksMatching(state, this.fromScope, this.fromTarget)\n            var matchesFrom = false\n            for (var f = 0; i < blocks.length; i++) {\n        sum += blocks[i].population\n    }\n    return sum\n}\n\nfunction prosperityIntraMeshCoeff(matrix, fromSid, toSid) {\n    if (!matrix || !fromSid || !toSid || fromSid === toSid) { return 0 }\n    var row = matrix[fromSid]\n    if (!row) { return 0 }\n    var c = row[toSid]\n    if (c == null || c === 0) { return 0 }\n    return Number(c)\n}\n\n\n/** Fixed footprint for corp/syndicate prosperity spill (not district pop). */\nvar PROSPERITY_IG_EFFECTIVE_POP = {\n    Soylent: 152,\n    Magrail: 148,\n    Brazos: 146,\n    Families: 44,\n    Edges: 42,\n    Sharks: 41,\n}\n\nvar PROSPERITY_DISTRICT_IDS = [\n    'Docks', 'Aurora', 'Vats', 'Railyard', 'Pitts', 'Limelight', 'Deeps',\n]\n\n/**\n * Within-district: every stratum block → every other stratum block in that district.\n * Sparse coeffs;     },\n    },\n]\n\n/** All strata wired in Docks trade spill (per-stratum multiplier × base coeff). */\nvar PROSPERITY_DOCKS_TRADE_STRATA = {\n    rich: 1.2, first: 1, second: 1, coop: 0.9, drone:
